# D-KILL3: inspection records runners before a provider exits

> Latest status: see Round 2 fix and validation below. Earlier results are historical.

Started from `473fe2941953` on release/217. The prerequisite check found
`6c50e145e Merge pull request #234 from MaxGhenis/fix/kill2-contained-217` in
`origin/release/217`. The D-KILL2 build report, review-r1 and merged C-5.5 were
read before changing code. No Git remote was contacted and nothing was pushed.

## Cause, as executed

The reviewer ran an unrooted shell provider which left a setsid'd lockf waiting
on a scratch lock and exited by itself. Finalization marked the attempt `failed`
without quarantine while lockf was alive. On the review host's macOS 26, the argument
buffer of a hardened-runtime Apple platform binary can hide its environment.
After the provider dies, lockf has no visible marker, group membership or parent
link to the attempt. D-KILL2 remembers the kill protocol's first census, but
self-exit and a provider ending on a turn's SIGINT can reach finalization without
that census. Steady-state inspection previously remembered group members only.

Here, the unchanged release code reproduced both finalization decisions through
the real daemon methods with deterministic kernel observations: both attempts
became `failed` while the hidden runner remained. The inspection unit regression
also failed because `counted_identities` was absent. Two native cwd retention
regressions removed their owned worktree while our scratch observer was alive,
including the `/tmp` spelling; the owned-attempt census incorrectly verified
empty. The in-place cases passed before the fix. These are six intended baseline
failures across two separate lock holds; the between-inspection limitation passed.

The three real-daemon shell/lockf cases skipped: this sandbox denies `/bin/ps`
and both subprocess and in-process boot-identity sysctl reads. Native libproc cwd
reads of our own scratch children are permitted and were exercised. No live job
was targeted. The stop regression drives the real `TurnRunner` timers with a
controlled clock: SIGINT at 10 s, before close at 20 s or kill at 30 s. Its
deterministic variant is a `kind='turn'` job and rejects premature relay release;
the real shell variant uses the dispatch harness. A full conversation-service
stop through the relay was not run.

## Fix and contract

`Daemon._record_owned`, called by `_inspect_running` for both the shared-table
and fresh-table fallback paths, now also walks descendants outside the group.
An in-memory parent index costs one table pass, including deep trees; absent and
zombie parents remain walk anchors. Matching recorded survivors seed the walk,
and different births do not. New readable identities are persisted under
`counted_identities` with `attempt.counted_recorded`, merging with the stored
evidence. The next deciding census uses D-KILL2's existing persisted seeds.
Repeated observations write nothing. Only group members become owned; no
signal authority is added and no marker scan, responsibility scan or extra
process listing is added to inspection.

A deciding attempt census and retention now hold a job-owned worktree while a
live process has its cwd inside it. The native `PROC_PIDVNODEPATHINFO` read starts
no process; both paths are resolved before component-boundary comparison, so
`/tmp` and `/private/tmp` agree. Evidence says only `pid <pid>: cwd in worktree`.
Observers, including an operator's shell or editor, never become identity seeds
or signal targets. In-place workdirs and probes are excluded. Retention checks
before moving the tree and at holder checks on a moved tree, including resumed
retirements; a native cwd hold needs no lsof. An unreadable required scan holds
rather than verifies empty. An unreaped zombie must not create a global hold.

C-5.5, C-5.12 and C-13.4 and their change log are amended in the implementation
commit. C-5.5's D-KILL2 sentence now follows “Snapshot shapes from a different
birth are omitted.” “Its live state” again refers to the responsibility member.
Outside the separate owned-worktree cwd hold, a hidden-marker runner born and
reparented between inspections, before any
inspection or census captures it, remains a `documented_limitation`; both a
deterministic daemon test and a real-daemon test pin it. Cwd reads are snapshots,
so a process entering afterwards remains an observation window.

## Added inspection cost

Historical measurements from the first job follow. Their timing runs predate the
continuation's stricter lock requirement; new measurements run inside a lock-protected pytest hold.

Measured on this host outside the suite lock, comparing the original
`_record_owned` at `473fe2941953` with the new method: nine rounds of
1,000 calls, with stable recorded identities and no database writes. The
controlled table contains the host's native pid-list count of unrelated rows,
plus a guardian, provider and ten separately grouped walk members. Shapes are
synthetic because this sandbox denies ps; no live job's process details were
used. This measures the added in-memory inspection work, not the shared ps read
or first-discovery SQLite writes.

| Host pids / table rows | Before, median ms | After, median ms | Added ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1,220 / 1,232 | 0.0535 | 0.2762 | 0.2227 |
| 1,246 / 1,258 | 0.3135 | 1.0535 | 0.7401 |

The first run measured versions in blocks; the second alternated them. The added
time was about **0.22–0.74 ms per inspection** in the two observed runs. The
native cwd ABI was also checked against this process's own cwd. An additional
nine paired trials used 1,258 controlled rows, ten newly discovered walk
members and the real scratch SQLite store. Median inspection time changed
from 0.0632 ms to 0.5410 ms, an added **0.4778 ms**, including the evidence
transaction. Fetching/resetting the input row was outside the timing. The
runs reflect changing host load. The D-KILL2
review's 3.5 ms per full cwd scan is prior measurement, not a new measurement here.

### Lock-protected continuation measurement

The GIL CPython 3.14.7 benchmark ran as a test inside the combined suite-lock
hold, before the other test bodies. The host's native pid listing contained
1,635 pids; the controlled table had 1,647 rows (those unrelated row counts,
a guardian, provider and ten separately grouped walk members). Only the pid
count came from the host, not live job details. Nine alternating rounds used
1,000 stable-evidence calls per version; nine paired first-discovery calls used
a fresh real scratch SQLite store, resetting inputs outside the timing.

| Scenario | Before median ms | After median ms | Added ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Stable recorded evidence | 0.1599 | 0.7786 | **0.6187** |
| First discovery, including SQLite evidence transaction | 0.0455 | 0.4660 | **0.4205** |

## Tests

All counts below come from this continuation, on the merged production/test tree.
The lost job's positive counts are not reused. Documentation-only checkpoint
commits did not change the tested code. GIL CPython 3.14.7 (`Py_GIL_DISABLED=0`)
was installed with `uv sync --locked --group dev --python /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.14`.

Each foreground pytest invocation used its own `/usr/bin/lockf -k` hold of
`/private/tmp/claude-501/subfleet-suites.lock`, without `-t`. The first two
holds were serial; subsequent retention runs used four workers with automatic
worker replacement disabled (`pytest-xdist 3.8.0`, installed only in scratch).
`SUBFLEET_RETENTION_DORMANT` was unset, the installed-release `PYTHONPATH` was
removed, and plugin runs used only this workspace and scratch on `PYTHONPATH`.
`HYPOTHESIS_PROFILE=ci` and scratch storage were configured; properties retained
their committed example bounds (the long salvage properties use 20/24 examples).
No deep profile ran. The default uv cache was not writable, so uv used scratch.

**Distinct ordinary cases: 865 passed, 85 skipped, 106 unfinished**, of 1,056
briefed cases across 50 files. The additional inspection benchmark passed once.
All five intentional mutations strictly xfailed and are excluded from ordinary
counts. No ordinary case has an unresolved failure in its latest result.

Raw hold counts include repeated cases; the per-file table counts each original
case once using its latest result. Empty in-flight framework records are omitted.

| Hold | Actual result |
|---|---|
| worktrees-retention | 31 passed; interrupted after pytest reported 16m41s. |
| remaining-briefed-controls-cost | 425 passed, 42 skipped, 5 strict mutation xfails; stopped at its configured cutoff. |
| briefed-resume-1 | 266 passed, 43 skipped; interrupted with partial JUnit retained. |
| briefed-resume-2 | 74 passes plus 2 worker-interruption error records from the cutoff helper; both affected cases subsequently passed cleanly. |
| briefed-resume-3 | 72 passed; interrupted after pytest reported **21m18s**. |

**Hold-rule deviation:** the final hold exceeded the requested 20-minute bound.
The between-test stop waited on a long property case; the run was interrupted.
This was a controller error, not a test failure, and is not presented as a
compliant hold. No further holds were started at or after the 01:00 UTC cutoff.
Lock waits counted toward the job wall. The first command waited about 1h40m.

An initial cutoff helper raised inside xdist's protocol hook, which produced
worker-crash reporting for the completed large-copy and salvage-property cases.
The helper was changed to xdist's session-stop flag; the wrapper was changed to
wait for its child on interruption. Both affected tests were rerun and passed:
`test_ten_thousand_copy_fast_path` and
`test_retention_prunes_only_when_unbundled_unarchived_bytes_are_absent`.
Completed-phase journals and JUnit were reconciled by node id, and the four
production files used by the mutations remained byte-identical throughout.

### Mutations

Each targeted case first ran unchanged, then with only its production method
altered in memory. Strict xfail makes an undetected mutation fail the hold.
Patches were restored between cases; production source bytes were unchanged.

| Mutation | Named test(s) detecting it |
|---|---|
| Inspection not recording (the original release `_record_owned`) | `test_d_kill3_inspection_records_walk_members_without_signal_authority`; `test_d_kill3_an_inspected_hidden_runner_quarantines_after_provider_exit[self-exit]` and `[stopped-turn]`. |
| Cwd rule applied to in-place attempts | `test_d_kill3_cwd_holds_only_owned_attempts_and_never_becomes_a_walk_seed[in-place]`. |
| Cwd rule applied to in-place retention | `test_d_kill3_retention_does_not_hold_an_in_place_workdir_for_cwd`. |

### Skips and unfinished coverage

- **81 native-inspection skips:** 42 real-daemon cases require unavailable boot identity, and 39 process cases report sandbox-denied ps/sysctl. No ownership bypass was used. This includes the three native shell/lockf self-exit/stop/limitation cases; their deterministic daemon counterparts passed.
- **4 cache skips:** `test_retention_ordinary_trees.py` self-skipped because its offline uv install could not find `sortedcontainers==2.4.0`. These cases were attempted but not validated. The miss was identified after the final permitted hold, so no cache-primed rerun was started; this remains a verification gap.
- **106 unfinished cases:** 93 archive, 4 readback, 1 salvage property, 5 shared-folder and 3 sweep-race cases, listed exactly below.
- **Whole repository suite:** the other 455 test modules were not run. The required state-contract, module-name and merged connection-pool files did pass; there was no full-suite run.
- A full conversation-service stop through the relay, rather than the real `TurnRunner` timer plus deterministic daemon path, was not exercised.

## Round 1 fix, continued on 2026-10-22

This continuation starts at salvaged `48fb793ba`. The first round-1 job ended
on a transient Codex transport failure; its commits and WIP are preserved.
The audit of the brief's decisions at that starting point is:

| Decision | Starting status | Saved work and remaining work |
|---|---|---|
| 1: cwd-only evidence never changes verdicts | Partly done | `6074d72e0` separates containment from cwd holds, adds a 30 s finalization/start-grace wait, preserves outcomes and worktree leases, and names pids only. `f76bbd874` covers the review input, start grace and an observer leaving early. The unreviewed WIP adds terminal scan pacing; finish its review and validate it. |
| 2: bounded counted identities | Partly done | `6074d72e0` adds two-table expiry; `a0c1ac2d0`, `59b17b116`, `2925c66d6` cover restart and interrupted writes and persist first-miss state. Measurement and final validation/reporting remain. |
| 3: canonical case spelling | Done in code and tests | `6074d72e0` canonicalizes both cwd paths and retention's target; `f76bbd874` adds case-insensitive APFS regressions. Re-run validation. |
| 4: moved-tree and resumed-retirement rechecks | Done in tests | `f76bbd874` adds after-move and journal-quarantined restart observers; the existing rechecks are retained. Re-run validation. |
| 5: generated inspection property | Done in tests | `a87681227` generates 0–12 adversarial rows and alternative seed births, with ownership, remembered-walk, census, idempotence and expiry assertions. Adapt the identity-map traversal to #223's added scalar liveness evidence and re-run. |

The original merge `a1a3e3b26` brought in cached release `43f42d83d`, including
#212's frozen-stream finalization and #232's stopped-daemon port. At the new
job's start, the available `origin/release/217` still names `43f42d83d`, rather
than the newer release merge described in the continuation brief. No fetch was
made. Merge `474891215` therefore brings in the exact requested locally
available #223 head `caf67cf8b`. Its conflicts are resolved by retaining both
contract change histories and clauses, retaining #223's inspected wall-clock
ordering, and placing the cwd-only start-grace wait at its relocated decision.
Inspection combines counted expiry with #223's durable liveness timestamp:
each distinct table advances absence age and liveness once; repeated use of
that same table does neither. The two conflicted signal-audit fixture stores
retain the interfaces required by both branches.

Commits use workspace-local `.git-local` metadata with the original object
store as a read-only alternate, on `fix/kill3-self-exit-217`. The caller's
checkout and shared index are not written. Nothing is pushed.

### Causes, fixes and the salvaged WIP

1. **Cwd-only verdict loss:** finalization and start grace treated an unrelated
   cwd observer as an attributed survivor. The fix separates
   `containment_empty` from `verified_empty`: wait at most 30 s for cwd-only
   observers, then capture the attempt's own outcome and deliverable, retaining
   only its worktree lease. Evidence and notices name pids, never commands.
   Attributed group, walk, marker and responsibility members and unverifiable
   results retain their quarantine behavior.
2. **Growing counted records:** inspected command shells outside the group
   accumulated forever. Entries now expire only after two distinct inspection
   tables prove absence, zombie state or a different birth. An unreadable live
   birth is not proof. The bounded `counted_absent` map persists the first miss
   across restart; failed writes retry the same observation. Each deciding
   census keeps its copied seed snapshot. Keep JSON rather than add per-entry
   rows: the bounded record is small and needs no schema migration. #223's
   liveness observation is coalesced into identity writes, or written once for
   a fresh table, without advancing either clock on repeated use of that table.
3. **Case spelling:** raw resolved path strings did not account for the disk's
   canonical case. Both the cwd comparison and retention's target now use the
   same canonical spelling as admission. Case-insensitive APFS coverage passes.
4. **Retirement rechecks:** the after-move and journal-`quarantined` recovery
   paths already rechecked holders, but lacked tests. New observers enter the
   moved tree before deletion, including across a simulated crash; both tests
   confirm deferral and restoration rather than pruning.
5. **Generated coverage:** fixed trees alone did not cover combinations of
   parent order, process groups, zombie/unreadable rows and alternative seed
   births. The property uses the reviewer's strategy, 250 derandomized examples,
   and additionally checks two-table expiry and retention of live births.

**WIP disposition:** keep `48fb793ba` in full. Its terminal cwd worker is paced
by `inspect_interval_s`, including after live-holder and failed-scan returns;
its offer query starts from active worktree leases, and clocks are forgotten
with those leases. `818afa596` extends its tests to both job and attempt lease
holders and restart rediscovery. All six pacing cases pass. No history is
rewritten and no part of the WIP is reverted.

The hub's additional 2026-10-22 live evidence is relevant to the scope: two lost
jobs left 13 escaped processes stopped in state `T` for more than a day. Their
`uv run` parents held uv's shared cache lock and blocked every prune. This is why
an unbounded containment wait is not an acceptable response to cwd-only
observers. D-KILL3 bounds that wait while keeping attributed writers subject to
quarantine. Continuing stopped processes after termination belongs to D-KILL4;
this change does not implement that follow-up or widen signal authority.

The saved `a1a3e3b26` merge message confirms that #212's daemon finalization
merged without conflict, with both contract change logs retained. The current
finalizer retains #212's frozen-stream capture/replay checks and receipt-wins
behavior behind the corrected containment gate.

### Five invariants

- **Verdicts:** cwd-only observations affect only worktree release. The native
  observer/core finalization, start-grace and leaves-within-window cases pass;
  the separate real-daemon eight-second-provider case requires permitted
  process inspection and skips here.
- **No new signal authority:** counted identities and cwd holders never grant
  it. The signal audit in `test_census_cwd_outcome.py` passes; counted members
  still quarantine and terminal worktree workers exclude quarantined attempts.
- **Bounded memory:** counted identities are bounded by the live walk, including
  live recorded seeds, plus entries seen in the latest two inspection tables.
  Expiry, interrupted-write, restart and growth regressions pass.
- **Idempotence:** repeating the same table writes nothing, even after its
  observation clock advances. The generated property, merged-liveness cases
  and 300 repeated-table benchmark calls pass with zero transactions.
- **Birth identity:** a proven recycled pid is not adopted. Counted seed tests,
  recycled-entry expiry and generated alternative births pass. C-5.3's existing
  same-second birth-resolution limitation is unchanged.

### Current-head measurement

GIL CPython 3.14, real SQLite store, one foreground pytest through the required
suite wrapper. The synthetic table contains 1,000 unrelated rows, the guardian
and provider, and one or two command shells per one-second sample, each leading
its own process group. 7,200 virtual seconds cover 8,200 distinct command
identities: 4,100/hour for **two simulated hours**, not two hours of elapsed run
or a live Claude provider. The growth workload took 7.665 s.

before execution to explicitly pin `HYPOTHESIS_PROFILE=ci` and isolate failure
artifacts under task scratch. The corrected run acquired slot A at 13:28:41 UTC
and finished in 41.92 s: **34 passed, 13 skipped, four strict expected mutation

The 13 native daemon skips are caused by unavailable `sysctl`/`ps` inspection,
not by the harness's separate five-second start wait. Syntax checks also pass

### Final round-1 audit and validation

All five decisions are complete in implementation and committed test coverage. Decision 1 has passing native-cwd/core tests, while its separate real-daemon eight-second provider case skips because this sandbox denies the required process inspection. Decisions 2–5 pass their focused regressions, generated checks and mutation controls. The saved WIP is retained in full and its six pacing cases pass. Whole-directory execution remains incomplete.

The finalization batch initially had **295 passed and one failure**. The failing #223 test announced a new inspection while returning the same `ProcessTable` object. Production `procs.snapshot()` constructs a new table for every fresh read. `da0423c9b` changes only that test fixture to supply an identical-row, distinct snapshot, retains the original liveness assertion, and clarifies the daemon docstring. The failed case and both merged-liveness regressions then passed (**3 passed**). All 296 finalization-batch cases therefore have passing coverage; no production behavior was changed to silence the failure.

All five briefed batches were run through the required suite wrapper, one pytest process at a time, in GIL CPython 3.14. The same scratch venv was prepared once through `uv_sync_hold.sh`, offline, then reused with `uv run --no-sync`. Hypothesis used the bounded ci profile, with its storage and each run's TMPDIR inside the prescribed scratch folder. The five-second daemon-start wait is unchanged; no failure from that wait was observed. All 44 native skips are reported separately from passes.

The focused holds intentionally overlap. Deduplicated focused results are **632 passed, 44 skipped and four strict mutation xfails**, plus **one passing scratch growth probe**. Each of the four requested mutations fails its named assertion. A generated property counts as one pytest case and runs 250 derandomized examples.

The first whole-directory batch started at 14:40:04 UTC and stopped between tests at its configured budget, completing in **8m13s**, below the 20-minute hold cap. Its exit code 2 is the deliberate budget interruption, not a test failure. The complete collection is **13,405 unit cases in 337 files** and **2,142 fake cases in 115 files**. The queued second batch, with changed files prioritized, never started pytest and was stopped at 15:39 UTC, just before this job's 15:40 UTC cutoff for starting holds. No further holds were started. Lock waits consumed most of the available wall time; every acquired hold remained below 20 minutes.

### Baseline controls and verification gaps

The first job's saved `baseline-a8.xml` contains **11 intended failures and four passes**. Its `baseline_a8.py` reads the exact `a8aa6a07d` definitions of `_record_owned`, `_process_attempt`, `_finalize` and `worktree_cwd_pids` from Git and substitutes those functions under the newer harness. This is a function-substitution control, not a clean full-base checkout. The three cwd outcome cases, all seven expiry/boundedness negative controls, and direct raw-case comparison fail. A live birth remains kept, and the two already-correct moved-tree/recovery rechecks pass. The retention case also passes in that control because its newer caller still canonicalizes the target; it is not claimed as a full-base retention regression.

A fresh full-base checkout was not run in this continuation. The reviewer's original live rc-0-after-eight-seconds failure is supplied evidence, while this continuation's real-daemon version skips for unavailable sysctl/ps. The three native observer/core variants, actual lease retention/release, pid-only notices and deliverable capture do pass. The moved-tree tests are coverage additions for behavior that was already correct at the base; making them fail there would assert the wrong behavior.

### Cleanup

All task-started foreground test commands and suite wrappers have exited or
were canceled before pytest started. No detached runner was started. The added
observer fixtures terminate and reap their children. The required
`pgrep -fl 'suite_hold.sh|pytest|lockf'` audit was attempted, but the sandbox
refused the process list (`sysmond service not found` / `Cannot get process
list`); global process-list verification is unavailable. No unrelated process
was inspected or signalled.

The prescribed `sf-r240f1` scratch directory was removed with Python
`shutil.rmtree` guarded by the `/sf-` path assertion, including the venv, private
uv cache, probe scripts, XML/JSON evidence, hold temporary directories and
Hypothesis storage. Its absence was checked. Task-generated workspace bytecode
directories were removed, and no workspace `.hypothesis` directory remains.
Workspace-local `.git-local` metadata is retained for delivery. Final source
syntax checks pass. Only this report is changed by the final commit; source
behavior is unchanged after the passing recheck. Nothing was pushed.

## Round 2 fix and validation (2026-10-23)

Started at supplied head `7aacf2072d13`, including the hub's release/217 merge
at `c2e080e44`. No additional merge or fetch was needed. The caller checkout
was not written and nothing was pushed. Earlier report sections are historical.

### Four findings: cause and fix

1. **P2, publication fencing.** The terminal worker treated every worktree
   lease as a cwd hold, including leases an accepted job still needed while
   pushing. It now offers only non-in-place jobs whose export and push have
   settled and whose only leases, across the job and its attempts, are worktree
   keys. The same SQL predicate is rechecked atomically after the observer scan
   before deletion. This explicit readiness rule also rediscovers failed-scan
   holds after restart, which may have no recorded cwd_holders; keying solely
   on evidence would miss them. Other lease paths retain their behavior.
   Tests cover job/attempt holders, other lease kinds, unsettled export/push,
   in-place exclusion and a lease acquired during the scan. The real file://
   push test waits for `_publish_bundle` to enter before ticking the cwd worker,
   then verifies the fence survives and publication settles, for both allocated
   and in-place writers. The existing push/export matrix also gets that tick.
2. **P3, census cost during the wait.** Finalization and start grace ran a full
   census each control tick during the 30 s cwd-only wait. They now gate these
   reads at inspect_interval_s, measured from before the census, as the terminal
   worker does. Receipt/cancel handling stays above that gate. A result other
   than cwd-only clears its pacing; the existing attributed-survivor behavior
   is retained. Ended attempts forget their wait clocks. Tests include both an
   instantaneous read and the reviewer's 254 ms read cost. Native-cwd departure
   tests now advance to a due census, including departure within the window.
3. **P3, containment decisions.** Quarantine resolution and the wall's dead-
   guardian branch still used verified_empty, mixing cwd observers with
   containment. Both now use containment_empty. With cwd_pids={4500},
   --confirm-dead accepts and --force-release salvages as for an empty census;
   the worktree lease, cwd_holders evidence and pid-only notice remain while
   the observer lives. The wall takes `_lost` and completes its bounded wait
   without recovery killed_by or a kill event. Tests forbid both signal APIs,
   check salvage in both modes, and release only after observer departure.
4. **P3, contract drift.** C-5.9 now says the 30 s wait starts over after daemon
   restart and describes its cadence and terminal-worker eligibility. C-5.5
   names the terminal recheck after a verdict. The P2-2 change-log entry says
   counted_absent persists across restart, matching code and its restart entry.
   The restart test preserves the store and discards daemon clocks, proving a
   fresh 30 s wait. The amendment and change log were committed with the code;
   other merged clauses and entries were preserved.

### Six invariants, all covered by tests

- **Verdicts:** cwd-only evidence holds only the worktree and never changes a
  verdict. Native finalization/start-grace/departure, quarantine resolution and
  completed dead-guardian loss controls cover this.
- **No new signal authority:** counted identities and cwd observers never
  authorize or block a signal. The existing signal audit and new quarantine/
  wall tests forbid observer-driven signals; attributed survivors quarantine.
- **Bounded memory:** counted entries stay bounded by the live walk plus the
  latest two distinct inspection tables. Expiry, restart, interrupted-write
  and generated inspection tests exercise this unchanged round-1 rule.
- **Idempotence:** repeating the same inspection table writes nothing. The
  generated property and merged-liveness tests cover it. A fresh identical
  table may still persist #223's liveness observation, as before.
- **Birth identity:** a proven recycled pid is not adopted. Counted, recycled-
  pid, survivor and generated-birth tests cover the unchanged rule.
- **New, lease fencing:** the cwd worker never releases a worktree lease while
  another lease of the job/its attempts is held, or while export or push is
  unsettled. In-place jobs are never offered. Eligibility, race and real push
  controls cover this. The strict broad-offer mutation fails the named test
  `test_cwd_worker_never_offers_or_releases_a_job_with_other_leases`, through
  `test_round2_broad_terminal_offer_fails_the_other_leases_regression`.

### Census counts per 30 s window

Every measurement used suite_hold.sh and GIL CPython 3.14.7. The shape was a
native /bin/sleep cwd observer in an allocated worktree, with no attributed
members, 50 ms ticks and inspect_interval_s=1. Before controls used the exact
7aacf2072d13 definitions of _process_attempt, _finalize and _cwd_wait.

| Wait | Window | Before | After |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| Finalization | 30 s actual time | 171 | 28 |
| Start grace | 30 s actual time | 171 | 28 |
| Finalization | 30 s controlled clock | 600 | 30 |
| Start grace | 30 s controlled clock | 600 | 30 |

Eight measurement cases passed. Attributed population sources were injected
empty and the native cwd pid list was restricted to the test-owned observer.
These are cadence measurements, not host-wide latency measurements; no live
job's process was inspected. Native-cwd census medians were about 0.76/0.44 ms
before and 0.78/0.71 ms after (finalization/start grace). The full real-daemon
observer case was attempted and skipped for unavailable boot/process
inspection; the native-cwd/core counterpart passed.

### Validation method and controls

One GIL CPython 3.14 venv was built and reused. Manual setup used uv_sync_hold.sh;
every pytest/timing run used suite_hold.sh, one controller at a time and at
most four workers, with worker replacement disabled. Each hold used a bounded
cutoff, scratch TMPDIR, scratch Hypothesis storage and uv cache, ci profile,
no cacheprovider and no deep profile. SUBFLEET_RETENTION_DORMANT was unset;
PYTHONPATH named only scratch and the applicable workspace/base control.
Lock waits counted against the job wall. No hold exceeded 20 minutes.
The last hold finished before 13:21 UTC, ahead of the 13:32:09 UTC cutoff
for starting holds. No additional holds were started afterward.

The unchanged starting production plus new regressions produced 16 intended
failures and one pass. The briefed files, census additions and all host-push
files then passed: 1,045 passed, 45 skipped and five strict mutation xfails in
2m25s. An initial focused hold had 41 passes, one skip and one strict xfail.
Whole-directory execution initially completed with 13,992 unit passes,
2,055 fake passes, 135 total skips (including the two process files), seven
expected xfails and five baseline failures. The final publication regression
file passed all 12 cases, plus its separate mutation xfail.

Two early CI-layout failures were scratch-harness interference: changing
failed_keep.ROOT's basename changed the literal scanned by those tests. The
harness now keeps the default basename and exposes the original default only
during read-only CI-layout checks. All affected helper files were rerun and
passed. A base provenance check also needed /var and /private/var resolved;
the corrected control passed. No repository code changed for either issue.

Four ordinary-tree cases initially skipped because an installed wheel lacked
uv's offline index metadata. The setup wrapper primed pinned
sortedcontainers==2.4.0 and all four clone/hardlink/resume variants passed.
No dependency-cache skip is accepted as final coverage.

A clean archive of 7aacf2072d13 supplied base production. Only the new
host-push regression file was overlaid. Seven cases failed as expected:
the five existing failures below and both new in-flight writer-fence cases.
One provenance check passed, confirming daemon/importer/descriptors came from
the base tree. Both push cases failed specifically after publication entered,
with the writer-key assertion; both pass on fixed production and then settle.

| Existing case, unchanged at base | Result at both base and fixed production |
|---|---|
| test_daemon_qos_tools.py::test_thread_priorities_reads_every_thread_of_a_clamped_process | /bin/ps is denied with PermissionError. |
| test_descriptors.py::test_c16_6_the_kernel_ceiling_is_read_on_macos | Kernel ceiling is unreadable (None). |
| test_importer.py::test_states_map_from_rc | An uninspectable unfinalized pid stays running rather than lost. |
| test_importer.py::test_the_daemon_never_adopts_an_external_run | That fixture remains an additional external live attempt. |
| test_importer.py::test_a_live_claude_process_outside_subfleet_keeps_history_out_and_fences_nothing[True] | An unreadable marker leaves the session held by a legacy owner. |

The importer explicitly treats failed pid inspection as live in _run_is_live.
These expectations remain failures; no ownership or inspection workaround was
introduced. Remaining platform skips are native inspection/boot identity,
filesystem-image mounts, a socket-ceiling probe and an explicit local-repo
benchmark. No five-second daemon-start failure was seen in the initial runs.

**Evidence handling correction:** an early cleanup removed raw journals before
the report was safely saved, then stopped on a read-only fixture. Aggregate
results were preserved in tool output; fresh locked runs restored all exact
per-file results below. One slow cleanup was interrupted before resuming
tests. Subsequent journals were retained until this report was saved.
Restoration exposed four 5 s Codex-shim timeouts and nine properties hitting
the 180 s test cap. All passed on bounded retries, with unchanged example
counts and subprocess timeouts; generated cases were allowed up to 900 s
within holds shorter than 20 minutes. The last SQL property passed in 689 s.

This report is committed separately. Only named files are staged; no logs,
JSON evidence or bundles are committed. Shared Git metadata and the .git
pointer were read-only; commits live on fix/kill3-self-exit-217 in workspace-
local .git-local (use git --git-dir=.git-local). No history was rewritten.

A read-only helper was briefly started before the located brief's no-sub-agent
rule was read, then interrupted; it made no edits or test runs. Subsequent
work was done directly. No live daemon/store or caller files were written.

The required pgrep audit was attempted after test holds but the sandbox
cannot obtain a process list (sysmond unavailable). All known foreground
wrappers returned and fixtures close their cores/reap their owned children.
No detached process was started; the early helper is interrupted.

After committing the complete report as 35c4ab89d, the prescribed sf-r240f2
scratch directory was removed using Python shutil.rmtree with the /sf- path
guard. This removed the venv, private uv cache, Hypothesis data, test worktrees,
measurement scripts, logs and raw journals. Read-only fixture directories
were repaired only within scratch; linked files were not chmodded. The
cleanup command completed successfully and scratch absence was checked.
Fourteen task-created workspace bytecode directories were removed; workspace
.hypothesis and .pytest_cache directories are absent. Workspace-local
.git-local is retained for commit delivery. No further source changes were
made after validation. Final diff checks pass and nothing was pushed.

## Round 3 fix and validation (2026-10-24, continued 2026-10-25)

Started at the supplied head `9b423bd26`: the hub's merge of `release/217` at
`3b13b36b7` on top of the reviewed `3aa49607c`. No fetch or merge was needed,
the caller's checkout was not written, and nothing was pushed. Earlier report
sections are historical. The work ran in two jobs; "Two runs" below says which
results come from which.

### Two findings: cause and fix

1. **P2, a finished job's cwd hold refused writers.**
   - **Cause:** round 1 kept the job's exclusive `worktree:<folder>` lease
     while anyone had a cwd in its worktree. Submit refuses every in-place
     writer while that key exists (`_validate_conflicts`, `worktree-lease`), so
     `subfleet resume` of the job was refused with nothing to resolve.
   - **Turns, observed at 9b423bd26:** a writable turn into that worktree was
     not refused at submit, as the review inferred. It was submitted and then
     held at admission `lease-held` on the kept key, for as long as the
     observer stayed.
   - **Fix:** where a writable job ends with cwd observers, the transaction
     that releases its other leases turns each `worktree:` key of the job or
     that attempt into `worktree-read:<same folder>:<job id>`, held by
     `cwd:<job id>` (`folders.CWD`, `Daemon._keep_for_cwd`). The four places a
     writable job ends are converted: its final export, a verdict without an
     accepted attempt, an unlaunched attempt with no attempts left, and
     quarantine resolution. A job queued again after an unlaunched attempt is
     still the writer there and keeps its own key.
   - **The worker** (`CWD_WORKTREE_HOLDS`, `_release_worktree_hold`) now
     releases only rows held by `cwd:<job id>`: never a `worktree:` key, so
     never a lease a live part of a job needs (round 2's invariant holds by
     construction), and never another job's row on the folder. It offers a job
     only once it has ended and it and its attempts hold nothing else, reads
     that again inside the releasing transaction, and paces its scans at
     inspect_interval_s as before. The query starts from the reader rows' key
     range on the primary key.
   - **Unchanged:** no refusal was added, C-6.5's index is as it was, and
     every other lease path is untouched.
2. **P3, the receipt-less loss path took two censuses a pass.**
   - **Cause:** on a dead guardian, `_inspect_running` took a census, then
     `_lost` wrote `attempt.lost_end` and `_finalize(lost=True)` took its own
     paced census, on every inspection pass of the 30 s cwd wait.
   - **Fix:** the census and the `_lost` call wait for the attempt's cwd
     clock, as the hub specified. `_lost` hands that census to `_finalize`,
     which decides from it instead of taking a second. The clock runs from
     when the inspection fell due, so the next inspection finds the census
     due rather than skipping an interval. `_lost` records the end only while
     `finished_at` is unset; COALESCE already kept the first end, so later
     passes wrote events and nothing else. The wall's dead-guardian branch
     hands its census over the same way.

### The key collision, and the choice

A detached job holds `worktree-read:<run folder>:<job id>`, held by the job,
for its lease's lifetime. For an allocated writer the run folder is its
worktree, so the kept row's key is that same key.

**Chosen: a distinct holder on the job's own reader key.** The two rows are
never needed at once, and each release can only take its own row:

- **The handover is one transaction.** The lifetime row goes with the job's
  other leases first. Then `INSERT OR REPLACE` writes the kept row, and the
  `worktree:` key goes. There is no moment with neither row, and the replace
  can take nothing a live part of the job holds.
- **The releases are disjoint.** Every holder-keyed release of the job
  (`holder IN (job, attempt)`) misses `cwd:<job id>`. The cwd worker deletes
  only `cwd:<job id>`, so it never takes the lifetime row or a resume's rows.
- **The worker waits for both conditions.** It offers the row only once the
  job has ended and holds nothing else, and no observer is left.
- **Admission never overwrites the kept row.** If the same job id were ever
  admitted again, its lifetime row's key would be held by another holder, so
  it would be contested and the job would wait for the observer to leave.

**Why not the alternatives:**

- **Holder = the job id (one row):** `PENDING_EXPORTS` (an accepted job
  holding any non-`worktree:` lease) would re-offer an export every tick.
  Every holder-keyed release of the job would also drop the hold. And the
  lifetime row is on the canonical run folder, where the kept row must be on
  the `worktree:` key's own folder (`jobs.worktree`, which retention's
  `turn-folder` pin compares).
- **A distinct key:** the key's id part would no longer name the job, which
  `folders.parse` returns and `_describe_lease` names as the job.

Retention keeps the job under the kept row through its existing `turn-folder`
pin, at selection and at commit (C-8.4, I5), and its own cwd checks are
unchanged.

### Five invariants, each tested

1. **Retention safety:** no folder with a live cwd observer is removed by
   retention. Retention's own cwd checks hold it, before and after this round.
   The kept reader row holds it after the observer has left and until the
   worker's recheck.
   - `test_r3_retention_keeps_the_folder_while_the_reader_row_lives_and_takes_it_after`
   - the interleaving property
   - round 1's retention cwd tests
2. **No idle refusal:** a finished job's cwd hold never refuses or contests an
   in-place writer.
   - the resume, turn and restart regressions
   - the interleaving property
   - the unit conversion tests at every ending
3. **Writer exclusivity:** two live in-place writers never hold one folder at
   once.
   - `test_r3_a_second_writer_is_still_refused_while_the_resume_holds_its_key`
     covers a detached writer and a turn beside a live resume.
   - the interleaving property
4. **Signals:** cwd observers never authorize or block a signal.
   - Round 2's quarantine and wall tests forbid both signal functions.
   - `test_r3_quarantine_resolution_converts_the_hold` forbids
     `signal_group`.
   - This round changes no signal path.
5. **Pacing:** each 30 s wait, the loss path included, takes at most one census
   per inspect_interval_s.
   - `test_r3_a_receipt_less_loss_with_an_observer_is_paced`, at 0 ms and
     254 ms per census
   - round 2's finalization, start-grace and terminal-recheck pacing tests

**The interleaving property** (`test_census_round3_interleavings.py`) runs
12 derandomized examples of up to 8 steps, plus 4 pinned with `@example`. It
uses the real submit, admission, finalization, export, worker, retention pass
and restart, and checks invariants 1–3 after every step.

### The loss path's censuses per 30 s

The probe ran the receipt-less loss path with one cwd observer and a dead
guardian. It used a controlled clock, 50 ms control ticks, and every tick
offered (the worst case). The first run ran it once at 9b423bd26 (an archive
export) and once at the fix. The continuation re-ran it at the fix, in hold
X2 (below), and got the same four rows. It did not re-run the 9b423bd26 rows.

| Code | inspect_interval_s | Cost per census | Censuses in 30 s | `attempt.lost_end` | Bound (30 / interval + 1) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 9b423bd26 (first run) | 1.0 | 0 ms | 56 | 31 | 31 |
| 9b423bd26 (first run) | 1.0 | 254 ms | 56 | 31 | 31 |
| 9b423bd26 (first run) | 0.5 | 0 ms | 112 | 61 | 61 |
| 9b423bd26 (first run) | 0.5 | 254 ms | 108 | 55 | 61 |
| fix (both runs) | 1.0 | 0 ms | 30 | 1 | 31 |
| fix (both runs) | 1.0 | 254 ms | 30 | 1 | 31 |
| fix (both runs) | 0.5 | 0 ms | 60 | 1 | 61 |
| fix (both runs) | 0.5 | 254 ms | 60 | 1 | 61 |

- **Before:** the review measured 60 censuses and 31 events at the same
  shape; the probe's 56 and 31 agree.
- **After:** every case ends `lost` at the first due census after 30 s, with
  `cwd_holders` recorded (`pid 4500: cwd in worktree`) and no `killed_by`.
- **Not measured:** a real-daemon cadence measurement was not repeated this
  round.

### Two runs

- **The first run** (job `20261023-195458-pr240-fix3`, 2026-10-24 12:40Z to
  17:46Z) made the five commits listed under "Commits and cleanup" and drafted
  this section. A network loss ended it before it committed the draft.
- **A continuation** (job `20261024-193041-pr240-fix3b`, from 2026-10-25
  02:15Z) started at the first run's last commit, `59f308dfd`.
  - It read each of the five commits against the brief and kept all of them
    unchanged.
  - It applied the uncommitted draft. That was this section, plus one wording
    fix in the change-log entry ("the transaction that ends the job" became
    "the transaction that releases its other leases": an accepted job is
    converted in its export's transaction, after its verdict).
  - It re-ran the checks below.
- **Which results stand:** the first run's holds ran at load 35 to 211, and
  after the fix its real-daemon census cases failed, nearly all at the
  harness's start wait. Where the continuation re-ran a file, its results replace the first
  run's. Results kept from the first run are marked so.

### Validation in the continuation

- **Setup:** one GIL CPython 3.14.7 venv, built once through
  `uv_sync_hold.sh`. The first run's venv was an editable install of its own
  workspace, so it was not reused.
- **Every pytest went through `suite_hold.sh`:** one at a time, no xdist,
  `-p no:cacheprovider`, a scratch `TMPDIR` and Hypothesis storage, and
  `SUBFLEET_RETENTION_DORMANT` and `PYTHONPATH` unset.
  - Each ran the venv's `python -m pytest` rather than `uv run`, so a hold
    could not stall on the uv cache lock.
  - Each ran under a foreground watchdog (`run_cut`) that sends SIGINT at
    18 min, then SIGKILL 90 s later.
- **The lock's queue set the pace.** For most of the night the wrapper
  offered slot A only (free disk under 50 GB), with 9 to 13 wrappers queued
  or running whenever it was checked.
  Three holds ran before the 4 h 30 m cutoff:

| Hold | Queued | Took its slot | Load at start | Ran | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | 02:53Z | 05:03Z (slot B) | 46 | 17 min 49 s, cut | 150 passed, 13 failed, 6 xfailed |
| X2 | 05:27Z | 06:05Z (slot A) | 51 | 18 min, cut, then killed | 1,372 results, 4 failures (below) |
| X3 | 06:26Z | 06:42Z (slot A) | 30 | 17 min 58 s, cut | 897 passed, 1 xfailed |

- **One file list, in priority order:** the briefed groups 1 to 3 and every
  round-3 file, then group 4, then the probe. Next came the files that call
  what this round changed, then everything else. Each hold took up where the
  last one stopped.
- **X1 spent about 10 minutes collecting** the whole list, at load 45 to 100.
  It ran with bytecode writing off (`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, as in the
  first run), in a fresh workspace with no compiled bytecode.
  - Before X2, the package and tests were compiled once (`compileall`) and the
    flag was dropped, and later holds passed shorter lists.
  - X2 then collected in about a minute.
  - Load fell over the same period, so the causes were not separated.
- **X2's SIGINT did not end pytest,** and the watchdog killed it 90 s later,
  before it wrote its JUnit XML. Its counts come from its progress line,
  mapped onto files in command-line order:
  - each file's test count is taken from X1's XML or the first run's;
  - the mapping is checked by its xfails, which land exactly on the two files
    that have them (4 each);
  - it is used only up to the probe, past which counts for the files are
    unknown.
  - The names of its four failures come from the state roots the harness
    keeps for a failed test (`/tmp/sf-failed/<pytest pid>/<test>`).
- **The contract guards** (`fake/test_state_contract`,
  `unit/test_test_module_names`, `unit/test_invariants_index`) ran first in
  X2, not in a hold of their own as the brief asks. With waits of up to
  2 h 10 min for a slot, a separate hold for them might not have started
  before the cutoff.

### Results at 9b423bd26: the first run's, not re-run

The continuation built an archive export of 9b423bd26 with the round-3 test
files beside it, but had no hold left to run it. The rows below are the first
run's.

No pre-existing failure was found in any briefed file at 9b423bd26.

| Hold | Files | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1 (workspace) | `unit/test_census_counted`, `fake/test_census_self_exit_daemon`, `_inspection_finalization`, `_escapers_daemon`, `_cwd_outcome`, `unit/test_census_round2` (+ mutation), `_worktree_recheck_pacing`, `unit/test_retention_cwd_rechecks` | 54 passed, 1 strict xfail |
| 2 (workspace) | `unit/test_procs`, `_procs_cwd`, `_retention_worktree_cwd`, `process/test_census_hidden_environment_process` | 81 passed, 4 xfailed |
| 3 (workspace) | `unit/test_census*.py` (15 files), `fake/test_census_recycled_pid_daemon`, `process/test_census_responsibility_process` | 252 passed, 5 strict xfails |
| 4 (export) | brief-fix1's fourth group and the 21 `unit/test_host_push*` files | 692 passed, 1 skipped (local-repo benchmark) |
| 4 (export) | the round-3 test files, new and updated | 42 failed (expected), 20 passed, 1 xfailed |

**The 42 expected failures at 9b423bd26, by cause:**

- **The resume regressions** failed with the review's `AdapterError: worktree
  has a lease`.
- **The turn regression** failed with `lease-held` on the kept
  `worktree:<folder>`. So at 9b423bd26 a writable turn into such a worktree
  was not refused at submit, as the review inferred: it was held at
  admission for as long as the observer stayed.
- **The loss-path pacing test** counted 112 and 108 censuses against a bound
  of 61. The departure test recorded 2 `attempt.lost_end` events.
- **The interleaving property** failed on its first one-step case,
  `['end-observed']`: the finished source still held its `worktree:` key.
  (That export's copy of the property had Hypothesis's shrink phase removed;
  the committed test keeps it.)
- **The mutation control** failed for want of `Daemon._keep_for_cwd`.
- **The rest** failed for want of `folders.cwd_holder`, or because they
  asserted the reader-row shape.
- **The 20 passes at 9b423bd26** are guards that also held then:
  - an unlaunched job queued again keeps its key;
  - an export with no observer releases everything;
  - round 1 and 2 cases this round left unchanged: the worker never takes a
    job with other leases, an in-place writer or unsettled publication; the
    wall and wait pacing; an observer leaving within the window; evidence
    grants no signal.
- **Not run at 9b423bd26:** the contract-guard hold.

**Fixes to round-3 tests that the first run's holds prompted:**

- **0514848ff:** a writable turn beside a live resume is held at admission,
  not refused.
- **6aa0769d2:** a second detached writer is refused by submit's
  live-writer check (`worktree-writer`), which runs before the lease read.
- Both are test expectations; no production change followed from them.

## Round 4 fix and validation (2026-10-25)

Started at the supplied head `f4f507e1d`: the hub's merge of `release/217`
at `8f1c65f69` (#221) on top of the reviewed `4cddd7925`. No fetch or merge
was needed, the caller's checkout was not written, and nothing was pushed.
Earlier report sections are historical. The work ran in two jobs; "Two runs"
below says which results come from which.

### Three findings and a note: cause and fix

1. **P3-1, a job that ended from the queue dropped its worktree lease.**
   - **Cause:** start grace that ends an attempt unlaunched, finding only a
     cwd observer, with attempts left, queues the job again holding its
     `worktree:<folder>` key (round 3, by design). Three endings then ran
     `DELETE FROM leases WHERE holder=<job>` and took no cwd scan: a cancel
     while the job waits (`_request_cancel`, no active attempt),
     `_fail_queued`, and every `_unlaunched` caller that passes no census:
     four in `_launch` (cancelled before launch, a launch check that got no
     answer, "needs a shell", guardian identity unavailable) and
     `_process_attempt`'s reserved attempt that never launched. The
     folder was left with no row and the notice without its `worktree
     retained` line.
   - **Fix (r4's first option):** one rule, `_release_ending`, used by all
     three. Before the transaction, the ending scans the worktree
     (`_ending_observers` for `_fail_queued` and `_unlaunched`; one family
     query in `_request_cancel` for each job that holds a `worktree:` key and
     has no active attempt). In the transaction it releases the job's other
     leases and, unless the scan showed no observer, converts the key with
     `_keep_for_cwd`, exactly as the export does. An unreadable scan, or a job
     that held no key when asked but holds one by its transaction, keeps the
     row; the cwd worker's recheck releases it. The notice gets the
     `worktree retained` line, pids only. A job queued again (`_unlaunched`
     with attempts left) keeps its own key, as after start grace. Start
     grace's census still decides its own ending.
   - **One other queue ending, judged from code:** admission's
     `_skip_revive` also deletes a queued job's leases by holder, inside
     admission's transaction. Revives are always in place
     (`sessions/revive.py`, `in_place=True`), and `owned_worktree` returns
     None for an in-place job, so no census or scan ever finds an observer
     for one and no revive is queued again holding a kept key. It is
     unchanged.
2. **P3-2, an observer held #246's attempt TMPDIR.**
   - **Cause:** round 1 made `Containment.verified_empty` require no cwd
     observers. #246's `_release_tmpdir` treated a census that was not
     `verified_empty` as uncertain: it kept the TMPDIR, recorded no hold, and
     every sweep took a census again until the shell left.
   - **Fix:** `_release_tmpdir` decides from `census.containment_empty` (no
     attributed process, and verifiable). An observer-only census removes
     the TMPDIR at the verdict, and no sweep asks again. The attempt's own
     attributed processes still hold it (`_tmpdir_held`, no new census until
     its state changes); an unverifiable census, including one whose cwd
     read failed (`_contain` marks it unverifiable), is still asked again.
     `AttemptTemp.remove`'s `verified_empty` argument is now documented as
     that.
3. **P3-3, two contract sentences.** C-5.9's quarantine sentence takes r4's
   wording ("the worktree stays held, as a reader row (C-5.5)"). The round-3
   change-log entry now says round 1 refused a resume at submit with
   `worktree-lease` and held a writable turn at admission with
   `lease-held`.
4. **r4's note, the extra census:** `_unlaunched` hands start grace's census
   to `_cleanup_attempt_tmpdir`, which no longer takes a fresh one. The
   decision is the same; the ending takes one census, not two.
5. **Found by this round's pacing test, outside the brief's decisions:**
   - **Input:** the receipt-less loss path with an observer, a dead guardian,
     a shared process table that expires 0.1 s after it falls due (another
     attempt's read, C-5.12), 50 ms ticks, and a 254 ms census.
   - **Counterexample at `f4f507e1d`:** 90 censuses in 30 s at
     inspect_interval_s 0.5, against Invariant 5's bound of 61 (the
     `[review-cost]` case of the pacing test).
   - **Cause:** the loss path's cwd clock ran from when its inspection fell
     due. With an early-expiring table the due time advances 0.1 s per
     inspection, while each pass with a census costs about 0.3 s of real
     time, so the due time lags real time and a clock set from it can
     already be past when it is set.
   - **Smallest change:** `_cwd_wait` sets the clock to
     `max(began, previous clock) + inspect_interval_s`, so it never restarts
     sooner than an interval after the clock that allowed the census before
     it. Finalization and start grace pass the census's own start, taken
     after `_cwd_census_due` allowed it, so it is never before that clock and
     their cadence is unchanged. C-5.9 states the rule. Nothing in the hub's
     decisions changed; this keeps Invariant 5 true on an input r4 did not
     run.

pids its census found).

### Seven invariants, each tested

1. **Retention safety** (round 3, unchanged): no folder with a live cwd
   observer is removed by retention. Round 3's retention test, the
   interleaving property and round 1's retention cwd tests.
2. **No idle refusal** (round 3): a finished job's cwd hold never refuses or
   contests an in-place writer. Round 3's resume, turn and restart
   regressions and the interleaving property; the reader row this round
   writes at the queue endings is the same row.
3. **Writer exclusivity** (round 3): two live in-place writers never hold
   one folder at once. Round 3's second-writer test and the interleaving
   property; a job queued again keeps its own key, so no other writer can
   take the folder while it waits.
4. **Signals** (round 3): cwd observers never authorize or block a signal.
   The I6 property forbids `signal_group` at every ending; round 2's and 3's
   signal tests are unchanged.
5. **Pacing** (round 3): each 30 s wait, the loss path included, takes at
   most one census per inspect_interval_s. Round 3's loss-path test, and
   this round's shared-table test at 0 ms and 254 ms per census (the case
   that failed at `f4f507e1d`).
6. **I6:** a job that ends by any path while it holds a `worktree:` key and
   has a live observer leaves exactly one `worktree-read:<folder>:<job>` row
   held by `cwd:<job>`, and no writer key; with no observer it leaves none.
   `test_r4_i6_every_ending_with_an_observer_leaves_one_reader_row_and_no_writer_key`
   draws an ending from all seven (export, a verdict without an accepted
   attempt, unlaunched with no attempts left, quarantine resolution, cancel
   from the queue, `_fail_queued`, cancelled before launch) and 0 to 3
   observer pids. It runs 8 derandomized examples plus one `@example` per
   ending with r4's observer, on the real submit, admission, finalization,
   export and kill.
7. **I7:** an attempt TMPDIR is held only while the attempt's own attributed
   processes live or its census is unverifiable; a cwd observer never holds
   it. `test_r4_i7_only_the_attempts_own_processes_or_doubt_hold_its_tmpdir`
   draws every census source, observers and doubt (40 derandomized examples
   plus r4's census as an `@example`) through `_release_tmpdir`, and checks
   kept, removed, and `_tmpdir_held` against the rule.

### Every other reader of `verified_empty`

`verified_empty` is `containment_empty and not cwd_pids`. Only the TMPDIR
decision changed.

| Reader | Should mean | Changed? |
|---|---|---|
| `daemon._release_tmpdir` (attempts, probes and enrollments; the verdict's, start grace's and quarantine resolution's cleanup, and every sweep) | no processes of the holder's own | yes, to `containment_empty` |
| `AttemptTemp.remove(verified_empty=…)` in `attempt_tmp.py` | a keyword set by `_release_tmpdir`, not a census read | docstring only |
| `daemon._contain_probe` (five reads: the probe's kill protocol, its `contained` or `quarantined` state, its return) | no processes of the probe's own | no. `_contain` adds a cwd scan only for an attempt id, so a probe's census never has observers and the two properties agree |
| `daemon._contained_empty` (a probe record's saved containment) | no processes of the probe's own | no; reads `live_pids` and `unverifiable`, which is `containment_empty` |
| `retention.py` | — | no reads. Retention's worktree checks call `procs.worktree_cwd_pids` directly, which is "nothing in the folder at all" |

Every census that decides an attempt (verdict, start grace, loss, kill,
quarantine resolution, the wall) already read `containment_empty` before
this round.

### Mutants

Each runs as a strict xfail against its named test; each named test passes
unmutated. A mutant is applied to one `Daemon` method's source text, and a
companion test checks that its anchor still matches once.

| Mutant | Named test | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| Export conversion in a second transaction (r4: survived) | crash probe, `test_r4_a_crash_in_the_export_rolls_it_back_whole_and_the_retry_converts[after-conversion]` | killed |
| `_unlaunched` TMPDIR cleanup inside its transaction (r4: survived) | placement probe, `test_r4_the_unlaunched_cleanup_runs_after_its_ending_commits_and_outside_the_lock` | killed: the census ran at write depth 1 before commit |
| Same, before its transaction (r4: survived) | same | killed: the attempt was still live, nothing freed |
| The loss path's pacing gate alone (r4: survived) | `test_r4_the_cwd_clock_paces_the_loss_when_the_shared_table_expires_early[instant]` | killed: a census on every early-expiring table |
| The cwd clock from the due time alone (`f4f507e1d`'s rule) | same, `[review-cost]` | killed: 90 against 61 |
| TMPDIR release from `verified_empty` (P3-2 undone) | `test_r4_an_observer_only_census_removes_the_tmpdir_at_the_verdict` | killed |
| A fresh census in the `_unlaunched` cleanup (the note undone) | `test_r4_start_grace_hands_its_census_to_the_tmpdir_cleanup` | killed |
| Queue endings drop the key (P3-1 undone) | `test_r4_a_job_ending_from_the_queue_…[observer-*]`, three endings | killed |
| Worker deletes by key, not by holder | `test_r3_the_worker_releases_only_the_kept_row` | equivalent |

**Why "worker deletes by key" is equivalent:** the worker runs only for a
finished job that, with its attempts, holds no other lease, and it revalidates
that in its releasing transaction. The kept row's key,
`worktree-read:<folder>:<job id>`, is the job's own reader key on that
folder. The only other row that could carry it is the job's lifetime reader
row on its run folder, which went with the job's other leases in the same
transaction that wrote the kept row, and no other job's key can name this
job's id. So when the worker runs, the kept row is the only row with that
key, and deleting by key or by holder removes the same row.

### The loss path's censuses per 30 s, with a shared table that expires early

An uncommitted probe ran the receipt-less loss path with one cwd observer and
a dead guardian, a controlled clock, 50 ms ticks with every tick offered, and
a shared process table that expires 0.1 s after it falls due. At the head it
ran in hold H2:

| inspect_interval_s | Cost per census | Censuses in 30 s | Bound (30 / interval + 1) | `attempt.lost_end` | Ends |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 1.0 | 0 ms | 30 | 31 | 1 | `lost` at 30.15 s, no `killed_by` |
| 1.0 | 254 ms | 30 | 31 | 1 | `lost` at 30.32 s |
| 0.5 | 0 ms | 60 | 61 | 1 | `lost` at 30.15 s |
| 0.5 | 254 ms | 60 | 61 | 1 | `lost` at 30.34 s |

At `f4f507e1d`, the committed pacing test's `[review-cost]` case (0.5 s,
254 ms) counted 90 censuses against 61 in the first run's base run of the new
tests. The `clock-from-due-alone` mutant, which restores that rule, fails the
same case as a strict xfail at the head (H1).

### Two runs

- **The first run** (job `20261025-081916-pr240-fix4`, 15:08Z to 18:48Z) ran
  the base holds at `f4f507e1d` from an archive export, wrote the tests and
  the fix, and committed `d7ec5e524` and `91fc5ca0b`. A network loss on the
  host ended it, and a second attempt at 19:17Z ended the same way, before
  it committed its contract draft or wrote this section. Its scratch folder
  survived and holds its logs and JUnit XML.
- **A continuation** (job `20261025-131020-pr240-fix4-cont`, from 20:20Z)
  started at `91fc5ca0b` in a fresh workspace.
  - It applied the saved contract draft with `git apply`, audited it, made
    the two changes listed above, and committed it (`43d175fa4`).
  - It read both commits against the brief and kept them unchanged.
  - It wrote this section and ran the holds below.
- **Which results stand:** the continuation's holds at the head replace the
  first run's head hold where both ran a file. The base results are all the
  first run's, read from its logs and XML; B1, a re-run of the new tests at
  the base under the lock, did not run ("Not run").

### Validation in the continuation

- **Setup:** one GIL CPython 3.14.7 venv, built once through
  `uv_sync_hold.sh`, and an archive export of `43d175fa4` (the code is
  `91fc5ca0b`'s; later commits touch only `docs/`). The probe file was copied
  into the export's `tests/unit` only.
- **Every pytest went through `suite_hold.sh`:** one at a time, no xdist,
  `-p no:cacheprovider`, a scratch `TMPDIR` and Hypothesis storage, and
  `SUBFLEET_RETENTION_DORMANT` and `PYTHONPATH` unset. Each ran the venv's
  python on `pytest.main` in process, with a SIGALRM at 18 min 30 s that
  raises KeyboardInterrupt, so a cut hold still tears down and writes its
  JUnit XML.
- **The host:** load between 33 and 238, above 100 for most of the
  evening, so the wrapper often offered slot A only; free disk was 45 to
  64 GB. At that load a
  fake test that builds a repository took 15 to 43 s (H1's JUnit times),
  and the three guards took 8 min 9 s in H0 against 2 min 40 s in the
  first run's base hold at load 92.

**Every failure, by cause:**

- **The 26 real-daemon failures (H3 and H4)** are the same 13 cases each
  time: every real-daemon case of `fake/test_census_self_exit_daemon` (3),
  `_escapers_daemon` (9) and `_cwd_outcome` (1), the 13 that failed at
  `f4f507e1d` in b1.
  - 25 of them are `condition timed out after 5s` in `Harness.start`
    (`tests/fake/conftest.py:90`). Of H3's kept state roots, the
    continuation read two: one `daemon.log` held only its first line (the
    open-file limit) and there was no store; the other had no `daemon.log`
    at all. The daemon was still starting when the 5 s wait ended. This is
    the pre-existing harness wait, unchanged.
  - One, H3's `test_d_kill3_inspection_keeps_a_hidden_lockf_after_its_shell_provider_ends[self-exit]`,
    started its daemon and guardian and then timed out after 30 s waiting
    for the hidden runner or its inspection witness. In H4 the same case
    failed at the 5 s start wait.
  - Each teardown error is the harness's notice check on a store the daemon
    never created (`no such table: notices`), after a start-wait failure in
    `_escapers_daemon`'s `setsid_lockf_that_outlives_a_cancel` case
    (`[unrooted]` in H3, `[rooted]` in H4).
  - These cases run real daemons through inspection, cancels, the kill
    protocol and finalization. Some of that this round touched: every
    cancel runs `_request_cancel`'s new pre-scan query, and `_cwd_outcome`'s
    case finalizes with an observer through `_cwd_wait`'s clock, the export's
    conversion and `_release_tmpdir`. Those paths are covered in process by
    the round-3 and round-4 tests above. Round 3's X3 passed all 13 at load
    30; none passed here at load 34 or above, nor in the first run's `h1`,
    so no real-daemon case is verified at this head.
- **H0's one failure,**
  `fake/test_state_contract.py::test_c16_1_state_socket_handler_recovers_after_malformed_line`,
  was a 2 s socket read timing out while the handler answered
  `daemon.status`, at load 119 to 163. It passed at `f4f507e1d` (b0) and
  passed when H3 re-ran it. This round changed nothing on that path.

## Round 5 fix and validation (2026-10-26)

Started at `a2380e42f`: the hub's merge of `release/217` at `946ea9f96`
(#259) into round 4's `600d75520`. No fetch or push, and the caller's
checkout was not written. No production code changed, and neither did the
contract: only two test fakes, a new guard and this section.

### The CI failure and its cause

- **What failed:** run 40251224112 at `a2380e42f`, 8 of 10 shards. Every
  failure was one of 39 tests, each on Python 3.12 and 3.14, in five files:
  `unit/test_conversation_handoff.py` (21),
  `unit/test_conversation_handoff_dispatch_races.py` (7),
  `unit/test_review_r223_conv_probes.py` (9),
  `unit/test_conversation_service.py` (1) and
  `unit/test_conversation_wakes.py` (1). Most raised `AttributeError:
  'Daemon' (or 'FakeDaemon') object has no attribute '_release_ending'` in
  `_request_cancel`'s no-active-attempt branch. The rest asserted a state
  that the failed cancel never wrote (`'queued' == 'cancelled'`,
  `'waiting' == 'cancelled'`, `0 == 1`).
- **Cause:** round 4 (`91fc5ca0b`, C-5.9 P3-1) made `_request_cancel` take
  a cwd scan with `_scan_observers` before its transaction and end a queued
  job's leases with `_release_ending` inside it, which calls
  `_keep_for_cwd` and `_cwd_notice`. Two test fakes borrow the real
  `_request_cancel` as a class attribute but borrowed none of these. One is
  the handoff tests' `Daemon` (`test_conversation_handoff.py`), which the
  dispatch-races file imports. The other is the service tests'
  `FakeDaemon` (`test_conversation_service.py`), which the wakes and r223
  probe files import. Round 4 did not run these five files locally.

### The fix

Both fakes borrow `_scan_observers`, `_release_ending`, `_keep_for_cwd` and
`_cwd_notice` from the real `Daemon`, beside the methods they already
borrowed. `_cwd_notice` is borrowed as a static method, as `Daemon` defines
it. There is no stub: the fakes still run real main-store cancellation.
`_worktree_observers` stays unborrowed. `_request_cancel` scans only a job
that holds a `worktree:` lease and has no active attempt, and these fakes
never make one.

### The guard

`tests/unit/test_fake_daemon_borrowed_methods.py` runs three checks on each
fake:

1. **Every name read resolves.** It parses each borrowed method's source
   (`inspect.getsource`, `textwrap.dedent`, `ast`), collects every
   `self.<name>` the method reads, and follows the names the fake also
   borrows. Each name it reaches must resolve on the fake, as a class
   attribute or as an attribute its `__init__` assigns. The only other way
   out is an entry in `EXEMPT`, `{(fake, name): reason}`.
2. **Every exemption is still needed.** An exempt name that the fake now
   has, or that no borrowed method reaches any more, fails.
3. **A borrowed method keeps the real one's binding.** This check is an
   addition to the brief. A static method borrowed bare would receive the
   fake as its first argument. Reading its source can't show that, since it
   has no `self`, and the fault would surface only on the branch that calls
   it: for `_cwd_notice`, only when a cancelled job's worktree keeps an
   observer.

Neither of the brief's two contract guards needs an entry for the new
module. `test_test_module_names.py` needs a basename of its own, which it
has. `test_invariants_index.py` covers the ledger's rows only. CI's shard
plugin places an unmeasured file by its checksum.

**Deviation from decision 2.** The brief expected one exemption,
`_worktree_observers`. At `a2380e42f` the guard found five more names that
borrowed methods read and neither fake has, all from before round 4:

- `_wall_exited_descendants`: `_request_cancel` reads it only for a family
  wall cancel, meaning `family=True` with a daemon `max_wall_s` request.
  The conversation service never passes `family`.
- `_moved_on` and `_withdraw_pin_notice`: `_notice` reads them only after
  its early return for a turn job (C-26.12), and the service cancels turn
  jobs only.
- `_salvage_summary` and `_held_line` (static on `Daemon`):
  `_earlier_attempt` reads them only for a job cancelled with no active
  attempt after an earlier attempt ended. The fakes' tests give an ended
  attempt only to a terminal job, which `_request_cancel` leaves alone, or
  to a job that is never cancelled.

With `_worktree_observers` as the only exemption, the guard could never
pass. The smallest change that keeps both decisions' intent is to exempt
these five, each with the reason above. That is what decision 1 did for
`_worktree_observers`, and it keeps decision 1's four borrowed methods
exactly. Borrowing the five instead would add methods decision 1 does not
name.

### Tests

The tests ran through the suite lock, one pytest per hold, each in-process
with an 1140 s alarm (`python -c ... pytest.main`, the scratch venv's
python rather than `uv run`). There is no xdist in the dev group, so every
hold ran serially.

- **Red, hold `red`:** at `f4837714d`, which is the guard with
  `a2380e42f`'s fakes. It ran the guard, `test_conversation_service.py` and
  `test_review_r223_conv_probes.py`, at load 130: 14 failed and 109 passed
  in 980 s.
  - The service file's 1 failure and the r223 file's 9 are CI's tests in
    those files.
  - The guard's first check failed for both fakes. Its second failed for
    both, on `_worktree_observers`, which nothing reaches before
    `_scan_observers` is borrowed.
  - pytest cut the first check's dict to `{'_release_en...`. `4d9fefc5b`
    made the message list each name. Evaluated in plain Python at that
    commit (an export, six AST checks and no suite, so outside the lock),
    it reads, for each fake: `lacks what its borrowed methods read:
    _release_ending (read by _request_cancel); _scan_observers (read by
    _request_cancel)`.
- **Hold `h1`:** at `ded9c4840`, the five files and the guard: 208 passed
  in 610 s. That is the guard 6, handoff 48, dispatch races 9, r223 probes
  17, wakes 28 and service 100. All 39 of CI's failing node ids, taken from
  the CI log, pass here, the state-assertion ones included.
- **Hold `h2`:** the three contract guards first, then round 4's test
  files (`git diff --name-only 8f1c65f69 600d75520 -- tests`), until the
  alarm: 346 passed, 12 xfailed and none failed, in 1137 s.
  - The contract guards: `fake/test_state_contract.py` 37,
    `unit/test_test_module_names.py` 1 and `unit/test_invariants_index.py`
    25.
  - Round 4's unit files, in full: `test_census_counted_expiry` 8,
    `_inspection_properties` 1, `_merge_liveness` 2, `_procargs` 52,
    `_recycled_pid` 25, `_round1_mutations` 4 xfailed, `_round2` 19,
    `_round2_mutation` 1 xfailed, `_round3` 23, `_round4` 10,
    `_round4_mutation` 7 passed and 7 xfailed, `_survivors_review` 14,
    `_worktree_recheck_pacing` 4, `test_daemon_settle` 68,
    `test_host_push_export` 12 and `test_procs_cwd` 11.
  - Cut by the alarm: `test_retention_archive.py` (15 of 109).
- **Mutants:** in an export of `ded9c4840`, three edits, evaluated in plain
  Python:
  - `_keep_for_cwd` removed from the handoff `Daemon`: the guard names
    `_keep_for_cwd (read by _release_ending)`.
  - `_scan_observers` removed from `FakeDaemon`: it names
    `_scan_observers (read by _request_cancel)`, and reports
    `_worktree_observers` as an exemption no longer needed.
  - `_cwd_notice` borrowed bare in `FakeDaemon`: the binding check names
    `_cwd_notice`.

  The handoff fake's other two checks passed.
- **Hold `h3`, the exemptions checked by running:** a scratch pytest
  plugin (`-p`) gave each fake class a `__getattr__` after collection. It
  covered pytest's basename module and the `tests.unit.` module others
  import, four classes in all. The hook runs only after normal lookup
  fails; it logged the name and raised the same AttributeError, so no
  outcome changed.
  - **Coverage:** the five files at `ded9c4840`, then the other 33 files
    that use these fakes, until the alarm. It reached only the five: 196
    passed and 1 failed in 1148 s. The alarm cut 5 service tests, all of
    which passed in `h1`.
  - **The log:** it holds only its header. No test in-process asked either
    fake for any name it lacks, so none of the six exempt names was
    reached.
  - **Not covered:** tests that build a fake in a child process
    (`tests/nonblocking.run_child`) carry no plugin.
  - **The failure:** `test_an_op_handed_a_fifo_answers_at_once_and_close_returns[conversation.handoff]`
    is one of those. Its child was still running at the 60 s bound, after
    printing `"handoff-source"`. It passed in `h1` (28 s) and again in
    `h4b` (41 s); the 1-minute load was 89, 58 and 66 at the starts of
    `h1`, `h3` and `h4b`. The plugin is not in the child, and the parent
    only waits on it. It is not one of CI's 39.
- **Hold `h4b`, at `7257869db` (test code identical to `ded9c4840`):**
  that test first, then round 4's other files: 84 passed, 4 failed and 4
  xfailed in 1145 s, ended by the alarm.
  - **Passed, in full:** `fake/test_census_round4_endings` 22,
    `_round4_endings_mutation` 1 passed and 3 xfailed,
    `unit/test_retention_cwd_rechecks` 3, `unit/test_retention_worktree_cwd`
    7, `fake/test_census_inspection_finalization` 3, `_round3_leases` 8,
    `_round3_interleavings` 1 and `_round3_mutation` 1 xfailed.
  - **Four real-daemon cases failed:** `fake/test_census_cwd_outcome`'s
    `test_real_daemon_rc_zero_after_eight_seconds_keeps_deliverable_with_observer`
    (its other 5 passed), and all 3 of `fake/test_census_self_exit_daemon`.
    All four failed at the fake harness's 5 s daemon start wait
    (`tests/fake/conftest.py:90`). Each kept root's daemon log was empty,
    meaning the daemon had written nothing in 5 s. The 1-minute load was
    66 at the hold's start.
    Round 4 recorded these real-daemon cases failing at the same wait
    under load. They use neither conversation fake, and this round changes
    no code they run.
  - **Cut by the alarm:** `unit/test_retention_archive.py`, 33 of 109.
    These are the same first tests `h2` ran 15 of.

### Not run

- **The whole suite:** not run, as the brief directs. CI's run at this
  head is the check.
- **The other 33 files that use these fakes** (`h3`'s tail). None of
  them failed on CI at `a2380e42f`. They ran there with fakes that lacked
  more than these do.
- **`unit/test_retention_archive.py`:** 76 of its 109 tests.
- **The four real-daemon cases above:** unverified locally at this head.
- **The red check of the improved message, and the mutants:** evaluated
  in plain Python (six AST checks per tree) rather than through pytest
  under the lock, because the lock waits were 20 to 50 minutes. Only `red`
  ran the guard at `a2380e42f`'s fakes through pytest.

- **Disclosures:**
  - The Bash tool moved each hold to its background mode at its 600 s
    limit. The run polled each hold's log in the foreground with bounded
    loops.
  - Two holds waited at once: `h1` with `h2`, and `h3` with `h4`. The lock
    allows two slots.
  - `h4`, still polling for a slot after 33 minutes, holding no lock, was
    stopped with SIGTERM to its own two pids. That process tree was the
    only one signalled. It was queued again as `h4b`, with `h3`'s
    timed-out test first; `h4b` took its slot at 07:55Z, before the run's
    3-hour mark.
